========================================================
  BSHH Attack S2 — Malicious RSU                       
========================================================

  t=5.000   ①  V0 <-> V1: Normal V2V heartbeat exchange
  t=5.000   ②  V0/V1 -> Malicious RSU: DSRC heartbeat forwarding
  t=5.010   ③  RSU -> Controller: legitimate aggregate (CSMA)
  t=5.100   ④  Malicious RSU stores V0 heartbeat for replay
  t=10.000  ⑤  RSU replays stored HB impersonating victim
  t=10.000  ⑥  Controller holds conflicting liveness -> faulty routing

  LOG FORMAT: RSUs printed as RSU<idx>(ns3=<id>), vehicles as V<idx>(ns3=<id>)
  Every selected malicious RSU executes STEP ④, ⑤, ⑥.

========================================
  PAIR: ATTACKER=RSU0(ns3=205)  VICTIM=V127(ns3=132)  OTHER=V121(ns3=126)
========================================
[t=2.000]  STEP ①  NORMAL V2V HEARTBEAT EXCHANGE
  V127(ns3=132) -> V121(ns3=126) : Heartbeat(Sender=V127(ns3=132), beacon_sending_time=2.000)
  V121(ns3=126) -> V127(ns3=132) : Heartbeat(Sender=V121(ns3=126), beacon_sending_time=2.000)

[t=2.000]  STEP ②  VEHICLES FORWARD HBs TO MALICIOUS RSU (DSRC broadcast)
  V127(ns3=132) --[DSRC broadcast]--> RSU0(ns3=205) : Heartbeat(Sender=V127(ns3=132), beacon_sending_time=2.000)
  V121(ns3=126) --[DSRC broadcast]--> RSU0(ns3=205) : Heartbeat(Sender=V121(ns3=126), beacon_sending_time=2.000)

[t=2.010]  STEP ③  RSU FORWARDS LEGITIMATE AGGREGATE TO CONTROLLER (CSMA)
  RSU0(ns3=205) --CSMA--> Controller : Heartbeat(Sender=V127(ns3=132), beacon_sending_time=2.000)  ACCEPTED
  RSU0(ns3=205) --CSMA--> Controller : Heartbeat(Sender=V121(ns3=126), beacon_sending_time=2.000)  ACCEPTED

[t=2.100]  STEP ④b  MALICIOUS RSU STORES A SECOND, EVEN-OLDER HB
  Attacker RSU    : RSU0(ns3=205)
  Victim          : V127(ns3=132)
  captured_packet : Heartbeat(Sender=V127(ns3=132), beacon_sending_time=2.000)  (from an EARLIER legitimate exchange)
  Action          : RSU0(ns3=205) keeps this even-older copy for a SECOND replay duplicate (STEP ⑤)

[t=6.900]  STEP ④c  RSU SUPPRESSES VICTIM'S GENUINE CURRENT HB
  V127(ns3=132) --[DSRC broadcast]--> RSU0(ns3=205) : Heartbeat(Sender=V127(ns3=132), beacon_sending_time=6.900)  GENUINE, CURRENT
  Action          : RSU0(ns3=205) DOES NOT forward this genuine heartbeat to the controller (suppressed)
  Consequence     : controller's only liveness view for V127(ns3=132) at t~6.900 will come from the forged stale duplicates below

[t=7.000]  STEP ⑤  RSU REPLAY ATTACK
  Attacker RSU              : RSU0(ns3=205)
  Victim/claimed_sender     : V127(ns3=132)
  Packet source             : stored STEP ④ heartbeat captured from V127(ns3=132)
  RSU0(ns3=205) -> Controller : Heartbeat(claimed=V127(ns3=132), beacon_sending_time=5.000)
  physical_sender=RSU0(ns3=205)  claimed_sender=V127(ns3=132)
  Stale timestamp 5.000 < current t=7.000 -> replay evidence
  Different physical sender (RSU not vehicle) -> impersonation evidence

[t=7.000]  STEP ⑥  FAULTY ROUTING CONSEQUENCES
  Controller now holds conflicting liveness for V127(ns3=132):
    Latest entry : physical_sender=RSU0(ns3=205)  claimed_sender=V127(ns3=132)  beacon_sending_time=5.000  [FORGED]
  Packets may be routed using stale/non-existent liveness
  Expected impact: packet loss, added delay, degraded PDR
  <- ATTACK SUCCESS

[t=7.000]  [KEY-EXFIL] BSHH-S2 sophistication: V132 (complicit) -> RSU V205 : session_key K_{V132,nk} + private signing key SK_V132 handed over (collusion)
  RSU now holds V132's real credentials -> can produce messages cryptographically indistinguishable from V132's own genuine self-report
  Sophistication: SOPHISTICATED [key-exfiltration] — RSU signs with victim's genuinely stolen key + fresh ts -> Stage-0 genuinely passes -> LW+TGN
[t=7.000000]  STEP ⑤b  RSU REPLAYS SECOND, EVEN-OLDER DUPLICATE
  RSU0(ns3=205) -> Controller : Heartbeat(claimed=V127(ns3=132), beacon_sending_time=2.000000)
  Controller now sees TWO conflicting stale entries for V127(ns3=132) under one identity
